[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 5.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '["qid","type","difficulty","key_db","stimulus_db","stem_db","choices_db","bank","explanation_id","style","steps","notes","known_problem","tags"]'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"qid":"PT158-S3-Q5","type":"Weaken","difficulty":"2","key_db":"C","stimulus_db":"Domesticated animals, such as dogs, have come into existence by the breeding of only the individuals of a wild species that are sufficiently tame. For example, if when breeding wolves one breeds only those that display tameness when young, then after a number of generations the offspring will be a species of dog. Therefore, all animals can, in principle, be bred for domesticity.","stem_db":"Which one of the following, if true, most weakens the argument?","choices_db":{"A":"Domesticated animals cannot be turned into wild species by breeding only those animals that display some wild characteristics.","B":"In some animal species, wild members mate more frequently than tame members.","C":"In some animal species, no members ever display tameness.","D":"In some animal species, tame members are less fertile than wild members.","E":"In some domesticated animal species, some members are much more tame than other members."},"bank":{"stimulus":"Domesticated animals, such as dogs, have come into\\n existence by the breeding of only the individuals of a\\n wild species that are sufficiently tame. For example, if\\n when breeding wolves one breeds only those that display\\n tameness when young, then after a number of generations\\n the offspring will be a species of dog. Therefore, all\\n animals can, in principle, be bred for domesticity.","stem":"Which one of the following, if true, most weakens the\\n argument?","choices":{"A":"Domesticated animals cannot be turned into wild species by breeding only those animals that display some wild characteristics.","B":"In some animal species, wild members mate more frequently than tame members.","C":"In some animal species, no members ever display tameness.","D":"In some animal species, tame members are less fertile than wild members.","E":"In some domesticated animal species, some members are much more tame than other members."},"key":"C"},"explanation_id":"36d09dd2-0238-4398-b978-e59168bafba1","style":"standard","steps":[{"row_id":"40d87d6b-7241-4bb1-8d14-2d14f7092136","position":0,"label":"Conclusion (Therefore)","body":"All animal can, in principle, be bred for domesticity."},{"row_id":"84b6f8bd-424a-4d23-99d6-bc8b77efe3ed","position":1,"label":"Evidence","body":"Domesticated animals have come into existence by breeding only the tamest individuals of a wild species. If you only breed the individuals that show they\'re tame when they\'re young, then through successive generations of breeding you will \\"evolved\\" a domesticated version of the animal."},{"row_id":"803a5983-bcc7-4f36-ba62-eed7a5e23a79","position":2,"label":"Evaluate","body":"How could the Defense make the case that \\"Nuh-uh ... not all animals can be bred for domesticity\\"? They could maybe say that for some species, none of the individuals are tame enough for us to start the selective breeding process. Maybe we\'re trying to domesticate a hyena, and we go through a litter of hyena babies, but none of them display tameness when young. If they we can\'t get our hands on multiple tame individuals, then we can\'t keep breeding tame-with-tame to get ever more tame versions of a hyena."},{"row_id":"c964c786-b430-4290-ad9a-d07a9f8d0e65","position":3,"label":"Goal","body":"Look for an answer saying \\"you can\'t always find some individuals that display tameness when young\\") or something that helps us argue that the conclusion is too universal, that some animals would not theoretically be eligible for the domestication process."}],"notes":[{"row_id":"ab8446c4-0b25-48dd-a6f0-b8af498ad570","letter":"A","body":"The argument claims that wild animals can be domesticated through selective breeding. Whether domesticated animals can be turned back into wild ones through the reverse process is a completely separate question. The argument is about the feasibility of one-directional transformation — wild to domestic — and the reversibility of that process has no bearing on whether the forward process works for all species. Even if domesticated animals cannot be turned wild again, that tells us nothing about whether all wild species have the tame members needed to begin the domestication process. This answer addresses the reverse direction of a process when the argument is only about the forward direction."},{"row_id":"c00a699a-f633-4f26-ab6f-759634e49a6e","letter":"B","body":"The frequency of mating between wild and tame members does not address the argument\'s core assumption. The argument claims all animals can be domesticated by selectively breeding tame individuals. For this to fail, there must be species where the process cannot get started — where no tame individuals exist. Whether wild members mate more frequently than tame ones is a practical complication about breeding logistics, not a fundamental barrier to the process. As long as some tame individuals exist, a determined breeder could isolate them for selective breeding regardless of the mating preferences of wild members. The process might be slower or more difficult, but it would not be impossible in principle, and the argument\'s claim is about what is possible \\"in principle.\\""},{"row_id":"d60f6eb9-593f-48cd-8344-d0afd8724180","letter":"C","body":"The argument\'s domestication process requires a specific starting condition: some members of the species must display tameness, so that a breeder can select and breed them. The argument then generalizes: since this process works (as demonstrated with wolves), all animals can be domesticated. But this generalization assumes that every species has at least some tame individuals to work with. This answer directly attacks that assumption by stating that in some animal species, no members ever display tameness. If there are zero tame individuals, the selective breeding process cannot begin — there is nothing to select for. The method is useless without the raw material it requires. This eliminates the precondition for domestication in at least some species, directly contradicting the universal claim that \\"all animals\\" can be bred for domesticity."},{"row_id":"fd61136d-1740-4e90-9dee-861159b64f34","letter":"D","body":"Lower fertility among tame members creates a practical challenge for selective breeding but does not make the process impossible in principle. The argument claims all animals can be domesticated \\"in principle,\\" which concerns theoretical possibility rather than practical ease. Even if tame members are less fertile, they are not infertile — they can still reproduce, just at lower rates. A selective breeding program would take longer and be more difficult, but it could still proceed as long as tame individuals exist and can produce offspring. The argument\'s real vulnerability is the assumption that tame individuals exist in every species, not that the existing tame individuals breed at optimal rates. This answer identifies a speed bump, not a roadblock."},{"row_id":"b842bf1c-b79a-4aae-948d-3a7aa3828afd","letter":"E","body":"The existence of varying tameness levels within already-domesticated species tells us nothing about whether all wild species can be domesticated in the first place. The argument\'s claim is about the universality of the domestication process across species, not about variation within species that have already been domesticated. The fact that some dogs are tamer than others does not address whether, say, a completely wild species with no tame members can be domesticated. This answer describes a characteristic of the outcome of successful domestication rather than addressing whether the process can succeed universally. It is about post-domestication variation, while the argument\'s vulnerability concerns pre-domestication preconditions."}],"known_problem":"Correct; padded.","tags":"emdash-heavy; pt157-158-register"}'}]

const vals=load("lineEndingMismatches").map(m=>{const f=load("final22").find(f=>f.qid===m.qid);const r=(m.kind==="step"?f.steps:f.notes).find(r=>r.row_id===m.expected_id);return `(${[m.kind,m.expected_id,f.explanation_id,m.slot,m.actual[0].md5,m.expected_md5,r.body,r.label??""].map(q).join(",")})`;}).join(",\n");
const sql=`with expected(kind,rid,eid,slot,old_md5,new_md5,body,label) as (values ${vals}),
ls as materialized(select t.* from lr_explanation_steps t where t.id::text in(select rid from expected where kind='step') for update),
ln as materialized(select t.* from lr_explanation_choice_notes t where t.id::text in(select rid from expected where kind='note') for update),
actual as(select 'step' kind,id::text rid,explanation_id::text eid,position::text slot,md5(body) h,coalesce(label,'') label from ls union all select 'note',id::text,explanation_id::text,letter,md5(body),'' from ln),
eligible as materialized(select (select count(*) from actual)=16 and not exists(select 1 from expected e where md5(e.body)<>e.new_md5 or not exists(select 1 from actual a where a.kind=e.kind and a.rid=e.rid and a.eid=e.eid and a.slot=e.slot and a.h=e.old_md5 and a.label=e.label)) ok),
us as(update lr_explanation_steps t set body=e.body from expected e,eligible g where g.ok and e.kind='step' and t.id=e.rid::uuid and t.explanation_id=e.eid::uuid and t.position::text=e.slot and md5(t.body)=e.old_md5 and coalesce(t.label,'')=e.label returning id),
un as(update lr_explanation_choice_notes t set body=e.body from expected e,eligible g where g.ok and e.kind='note' and t.id=e.rid::uuid and t.explanation_id=e.eid::uuid and t.letter=e.slot and md5(t.body)=e.old_md5 returning id),
r as(select (select ok from eligible) eligible,(select count(*) from us) step_repairs,(select count(*) from un) note_repairs)
select *,1/(case when not eligible or(step_repairs=8 and note_repairs=8) then 1 else 0 end) atomic_assertion from r;`;

Assign complete interactive Classroom lessons directly through the local lsat-u-lessons MCP server, verify the saved content and student visibility headlessly, and log the assignment. Use the Node CLI for other supported actions.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nFor drills, messages, student data, access, signups, due dates, unassign, and unsend, use `assign-lsatu-work`. On Windows, invoke its Node entry point directly instead of relying on Bash being on PATH.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n# LSAT U Platform Actions\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua\'s workplace is this brain workspace. LSAT U operations should run headlessly when a supported tool exists. The browser is for checking what a person sees.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Mechanism\\r\\n\\r\\nFor an interactive HTML lesson, call the `lsat-u-lessons` MCP tool `publish_interactive_lesson` with the student\'s exact email, absolute HTML path, and title. The local stdio server is `system/scripts/lsatu-lesson-mcp.mjs`; Codex registration is `lsat-u-lessons`. It calls `system/scripts/lsatu-admin.mjs post-lesson`, which authenticates with the existing admin credentials, posts through `mcp_post_classroom_note`, verifies the saved HTML exactly and student visibility, and then archives only earlier versions with the same lesson marker. A repeat of the current version returns the same item without another notification. Use `dry_run: true` when the student or replacement is uncertain; otherwise publish in one call. MCP tools newly registered during a session may only appear in a new Codex session; the direct Node command is the immediate fallback.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe HTML must be a complete document inside AI OS, under 500 KB, with `<meta name=\\"lsatu-interactive-lesson\\" content=\\"unique-lesson-id\\">`. Reuse the same lesson ID for revisions of that lesson; use a different ID for a different lesson. Inline the CSS, JavaScript, fonts, and visual assets. Preflight rejects nonembedded resources; do not bypass it or paste HTML into an ordinary sanitized note. The already-live Classroom renderer loads marked HTML in a sandboxed iframe with scripts and forms enabled. **Daily lesson posting is a content operation and never requires Lovable.** Only an actual app renderer change follows the separate app deployment rules.\\r\\n\\r\\nRoutine verification is headless: the publisher reads the new item back, compares its HTML byte for byte, checks that it is visible to the intended student, and confirms one active version. The result includes its route and SHA-256. Use Codex\'s in-app browser only when a visual or interaction defect is suspected or the page design or renderer changed. A teacher login cannot open another student\'s `/classroom/item/<id>` route directly. Do not claim a student-session visual check unless one was actually performed.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe student opens the full lesson from Classroom **Notes and recaps**, the in-app notification, or its Inbox link. Classroom Home may show a recent note as a “Read” row, but it has a four-row limit shared with urgent work, so do not promise that every assigned note stays on Home. This does not affect the note\'s visibility in Notes and recaps or its direct item route.\\r\\n\\r\\n## UI fallback routing\\r\\n\\r\\nThe current student verification route is `/teacher/students/<student-id>/notes`. Its “Posted to their Classroom” section shows active notes and labels archived notes as hidden from the student. Use the browser\'s normal controls for any unsupported action, then verify the saved state through a fresh read. In Codex, use the in-app browser, not Chrome. Do not rely on old `/teacher?ws=` routes or old browser-tool names.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Order of operations (every action)\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Identify the student** exactly (roster names include email; ambiguity → one\\r\\n   concise clarifying question). Check `students/<name>/` for context and\\r\\n   `assignment-log.md` to avoid repeats.\\r\\n2. **Do the action** through MCP or the headless Node CLI when supported. Use the UI fallback only for unsupported actions.\\r\\n3. **Verify it landed**: use the publisher\'s read-after-write result to confirm exact HTML, student visibility, and one active copy of this lesson ID. It archives earlier versions of that ID and leaves unrelated same-title notes alone. Check notifications where available. If verification fails, inspect current items before retrying; never blindly post twice.\\r\\n4. **Log it**: append to `students/<name>/assignment-log.md` (create if missing) with\\r\\n   date, action, item, and evidence; drill verifications also go to\\r\\n   `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Guardrails\\r\\n\\r\\n- Every action here hits **real students on prod**. Anything a student will see\\r\\n  (message text, homework instructions, note bodies) is written in Joshua\'s voice —\\r\\n  route through `wiki/teaching-voice.md`.\\r\\n- If Joshua\'s request fully specifies the action (\\"assign Lydia the conditional\\r\\n  drill\\"), execute without re-asking. If content is open-ended (a message body he\\r\\n  hasn\'t dictated), draft it and show him before sending.\\r\\n- Fail closed: if verification is ambiguous, say exactly what was and wasn\'t\\r\\n  confirmed. Never report an action complete on the strength of a click alone.\\r\\n- No student-facing action as a side effect of a different request.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Paid session credits\\r\\n\\r\\nUse `node system/scripts/lsatu-admin.mjs passes <exact-email>` for balances and `pass-detail` for evidence. An exhausted pass can be replaced with `pass-create <exact-email> --credits N --minutes 70`; dry-run first, then verify through a fresh read. Preserve exhausted passes.\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore manually restoring a Stripe package, inspect its `stripe_payments` row and the webhook delivery response. The 2026-09-27 policy returned `grant: awaiting_teacher` for intentional teacher approval. On 2026-10-02, Joshua approved and deployed automatic crediting for new validated, confidently matched package Checkouts, including delayed successes. Existing rows default `auto_grant_eligible=false`; historical, dismissed, and granted payments are not automatically recredited. Unmatched payments stay in review. Verify the live row and deployment before diagnosing a bug; an empty `booking_commerce_events` read is not proof of no payment. Evidence: `research/2026-10-02-celia-payment-crediting.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nAssociate any manual restoration with its exact Stripe payment/event in the booking pass notes. Reconcile the pending teacher grant prompt without adding more credits, using the supported payment-ledger operation after checking its semantics. Do not replay the webhook or invoke `admin_grant_stripe_payment` after manual crediting; either can duplicate sessions. A dismissed payment is not marked granted, and future reconciliation must skip it or explicitly honor the manual fulfillment.\\r\\n"}'}]

Use for lessons, notes, explanations, drills, quizzes, flashcards, and instructional feedback. Logistics emails and marketing use their separate canonical registers.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Write LSAT material in Joshua\'s selected teaching voice\\r\\n\\r\\nResolve the canonical ai-os vault from the workspace instructions. This project-owned skill takes precedence over older vendor skills with the same name that prescribe a writer blend.\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore drafting or revising, read:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. [Canonical teaching voice](../../../wiki/teaching-voice.md), especially the teaching-content register.\\r\\n2. [LSAT teaching voice standard](../../../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md). Read it in full when calibrating the voice; for subsequent revisions, revisit the relevant sections and examples.\\r\\n3. [Demonstrated copy corrections](../../../wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md).\\r\\n\\r\\nThe detailed standard is maintained in one place. Do not copy it into this skill or replace it with generic brevity rules, required catchphrases, humor quotas, or a fixed number of sentences per paragraph.\\r\\n\\r\\nEstablish the actual lesson topic, prerequisites, evidence, and Joshua\'s approved method. The conditional reference teaches how to explain; it does not add conditional content to another topic. Preserve complete ideas, logical strength, modality, and the selected scope.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse original intuitive examples. Explain the ordinary meaning before notation, show the intermediate reasoning, and give questions a real instructional job. Keep the teacher and reader thinking together. When useful, show a tempting interpretation and how its meaning fails. Fresh practice should require the taught skill and provide reasoned feedback.\\r\\n\\r\\nRead the draft aloud, check its inferences and boundaries, and apply the standard\'s review rubric. Review actual prompt/reveal states when writing for an interactive surface. Do not claim an identical voice has been objectively established; use Joshua\'s corrections to refine calibration.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis skill governs local authoring. Companion to [[revamp-list]] and [[choice-note-issues]]. Ordered by how much a student could be misled.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Content that belongs to a different question\\r\\n\\r\\nThese show a student reasoning that does not match the question in front of them.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Question | What is wrong | Evidence |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| PT108-S3-Q6 | The DB stimulus and all five notes are for a different question from the official one. | DB stimulus: the bass catch downstream of the power plant fell. Bank stimulus for PT108-S3-Q6: the algae population downstream *rose*, with choices about detergents, water temperature and dissolved oxygen. The notes talk about predators, anglers and cooler water, which match neither set of choices. DB key C and bank key C agree only by coincidence. Needs a source check before anything else. |\\r\\n| PT137-S3-Q25 | Evaluate step is from another question. | \\"Denise and Reshmi agree that people decide whether to commit a crime.\\" The speakers are Graham and Adelaide (chess computer). |\\r\\n| PT143-S4-Q18 | Speaker table and Evaluate are from PT137-S3-Q25\'s template. | Table headed \\"Graham / Adelaide\\" (speakers are Wong and Tate); Evaluate says they disagree about countries being \\"better off as democracies\\" (the issue is autocracies). |\\r\\n| PT135-S4-Q25 | A note is from another question. | \\"Neither Samuel nor Tova address a general trend of modern life.\\" Speakers are Lutsina and Priscilla. |\\r\\n| PT133-S3-Q4 | E note carries speakers from PT142-S2-Q7. | \\"Neither Talbert nor Sklar would agree with this statement.\\" Speakers are Sam and Meli. |\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Suspected mis-keys\\r\\n\\r\\nNo explanation argues for a letter other than `correct_choice`. The scan\'s 16 \\"key conflict\\" hits in notes were all \\"Yes, the author assumes this, but...\\" openings. Two related items:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **PT158-S4-Q22** (Weaken, d5): the breakdown\'s Goal predicts the \\"different epistemologists contradict each other\\" objection, which is choice D. The key (E, \\"much debate as to which thinkers were epistemologists\\") is right, and the E note explains it well, but the breakdown steers students to D and the D note then calls contradiction \\"a feature, not a bug\\". Rewrite the breakdown.\\r\\n- **Bank answer keys disagree with the DB on 18 questions**, all of them bank entries filed under a different PT, section or question number (\\"moved\\" in `revamp-inventory.csv` (in this folder)). I read five of these (PT116-S2-Q6, Q7, Q8; PT131-S3-Q16, Q17) and the DB key is correct in every case. So the bank\'s `correctAnswer` is wrong for these, not the DB. Anything that reads the bank\'s key (drills, analytics, the golden-survey inventory) would mis-grade them:\\r\\n\\r\\n  PT101-S2-Q19 (DB E, bank D), PT101-S3-Q19 (D/E), PT113-S3-Q21 (C/A), PT113-S3-Q22 (D/A), PT113-S3-Q23 (B/A), PT113-S3-Q24 (E/A), PT116-S2-Q4 (B/C), PT116-S2-Q6 (E/B), PT116-S2-Q7 (C/D), PT116-S2-Q8 (C/E), PT119-S2-Q19 (B/E), PT119-S2-Q21 (A/C), PT119-S4-Q8 (A/C), PT129-S3-Q18 (B/D), PT129-S3-Q19 (D/A), PT131-S3-Q16 (E/C), PT131-S3-Q17 (A/E), PT148-S4-Q24 (E/A).\\r\\n\\r\\n  Note PT113-S3-Q21 to Q24 all have bank key A, which looks like a placeholder.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Bank alignment\\r\\n\\r\\n- **PT119-S2-Q19** is matched to bank entry PT103-S2-Q24, which is a different question on the same topic (musical intervals; the bank one is about an isolated tribe\'s preferences). Its difficulty and features in `revamp-inventory.csv` (in this folder) are borrowed from the wrong question.\\r\\n- **PT101-S3-Q1**: the bank stimulus differs from the DB\'s in both speakers\' text (bank: election boards hiring extra help; Sharon concludes \\"apathy\\"). The DB text reads like the real question. The bank\'s choices for this question may not match either.\\r\\n- 110 explained questions have no bank entry at all, so there is no difficulty and no answer-choice text on disk to check notes against (for example PT119-S2-Q12, PT119-S3-Q3, PT131-S2-Q5).\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Empty or partial explanations\\r\\n\\r\\n| Problem | Count | Questions |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Explanation row exists with no steps and no notes | 2 | PT122-S1-Q9, PT147-S4-Q6 |\\r\\n| Breakdown only, no choice notes | 16 | PT102-S3-Q14, PT117-S3-Q2, PT117-S3-Q8, PT117-S4-Q3, PT117-S4-Q5, PT118-S1-Q9, PT118-S3-Q8, PT119-S2-Q7, PT119-S2-Q12, PT119-S2-Q24, PT119-S3-Q3, PT119-S3-Q20, PT119-S4-Q2, PT120-S1-Q23, PT120-S1-Q26, PT120-S4-Q17 |\\r\\n| Some notes missing | 8 | PT105-S4-Q14 (3 of 5), PT133-S2-Q25 (3, including the correct answer), PT107-S3-Q7, PT108-S3-Q16, PT111-S1-Q22, PT119-S2-Q15, PT146-S3-Q11, PT156-S2-Q19 (4 each) |\\r\\n| Placeholder text as a note | 2 | PT147-S4-Q4 A (correct), PT147-S4-Q3 D (correct): \\"(No authored tag or explanation for this choice.)\\" |\\r\\n| Notes but no Argument Breakdown | 224 | Heaviest in PT148 (33), PT111 (14), PT122 (11), PT116 (10), PT102 (9). |\\r\\n| Question has no explanation at all | 352 | Known (`is_explained = false`); the 772-gap plan covers these. |\\r\\n\\r\\nSeveral breakdown-only rows (PT117-S3-Q8, PT118-S1-Q9, PT119-S2-Q12, PT119-S3-Q3, PT119-S2-Q24, PT119-S4-Q2, PT120-S1-Q23, PT120-S4-Q17) are written in a different register from the LSAT Lab text: bracketed labels like \\"Rossi\'s argument structure\\", em-dashes, \\"i.e.\\" glosses. Transcription damage\\r\\n\\r\\n| Damage | Count | Examples |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Margin diagrams spliced into sentences | about 192 rows, almost all PT123-140 voice-B | PT132-S2-Q6 \\"Listing exact caffeine LCC -> ELC content would make it easier\\"; PT137-S4-Q15; PT135-S2-Q14 |\\r\\n| Notes broken into one-to-four-word lines (a diagram column that lost its table) | about 98 rows | PT137-S3-Q12 B, PT130-S1-Q13, PT135-S1-Q25 D, PT138-S2-Q1 B |\\r\\n| Two-speaker table flattened into interleaved prose | 12 | PT123-S3-Q3, PT123-S3-Q7, PT131-S1-Q23, PT131-S2-Q2, PT133-S3-Q4, PT134-S3-Q5, PT134-S3-Q7, PT135-S4-Q25, PT136-S4-Q19, PT137-S3-Q25, PT139-S1-Q2, PT140-S2-Q5 |\\r\\n| Literal \\"- paragraph\\" lines | 30 rows | PT123-S3-Q12 (between every step), PT124-S3-Q19, PT123-S3-Q23 |\\r\\n| Spaces lost around bold or italic words | at least 30 rows | PT106-S3-Q24 \\"aCurious\\", \\"islesslikely\\"; PT104-S4-Q14 \\"publishedbeforethe\\"; PT126-S1-Q12 \\"HQ -> EEquality\\"; PT126-S3-Q15 \\"askTheordore\\" |\\r\\n| Truncated mid-sentence | spot-found | PT125-S2-Q11 C note (\\"supports the argument\'s\\"); PT134-S3-Q3 Given step; PT138-S2-Q11 C note; PT158-S4-Q3 Evidence step |\\r\\n| Unbalanced `**` | 8 rows | PT101-S2-Q1, PT101-S2-Q21, PT102-S3-Q17 |\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. LSAT Lab leftovers\\r\\n\\r\\nText that refers to things students on LSAT U cannot see:\\r\\n\\r\\n- \\"try this one\\" with no link: PT102-S2-Q20, PT139-S1-Q5, PT140-S3-Q18, PT158-S4-Q25.\\r\\n- \\"click on the bold if you\'d like to see other examples\\": PT140-S3-Q7.\\r\\n- \\"watch the class recording, via the play button on the highlighter toolbar\\": PT132-S4-Q18.\\r\\n- \\"Welcome to Test 89, everyone\\": PT155-S4-Q15 (LSAT Lab\'s numbering).\\r\\n- \\"the sentence ends here with no continuation on the LSAT Lab page\\": PT143-S4-Q9 (a transcription note left in student text).\\r\\n- \\"As we analyzed\\" with no analysis present (the breakdown is missing): PT148-S3-Q19 A, B, E.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 7. Question-type labels\\r\\n\\r\\n`lr_questions.question_type` has **82 distinct values** for about 18 real types: the same type appears as \\"Must be True\\", \\"Must Be True\\", \\"Must Be True (Inference)\\", \\"Must be True (EXCEPT)\\"; \\"Paradox EXCEPT\\" and \\"Paradox (EXCEPT)\\"; 30-odd \\"(type inferred)\\" suffixes. Anything that groups by this column (drill filters, analytics) will split types.\\r\\n\\r\\nLabels that are plainly wrong for the stem:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Question | Label | Stem says |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| PT108-S2-Q13 | Strengthen | \\"most strongly supports the claim that Ramona and Martin agree\\" (Agree/Disagree) |\\r\\n| PT108-S3-Q20 | Strengthen | \\"provide the most support for holding that they disagree\\" (Agree/Disagree) |\\r\\n| PT109-S4-Q14 | Strengthen | \\"The statements above, if true, most strongly support which one of the following\\" (Most Supported) |\\r\\n| PT125-S4-Q12 | Parallel Flaw | \\"which one of the following is most supported by them\\" (Most Supported) |\\r\\n| PT125-S4-Q16 | Most Supported | \\"most vulnerable to criticism\\" (Flaw) |\\r\\n| PT113-S4-Q12, PT128-S2-Q3 | Necessary Assumption | \\"is flawed because...\\" (Flaw) |\\r\\n| PT125-S4-Q14, PT128-S3-Q12, PT151-S2-Q1, PT151-S3-Q18 | Weaken | \\"vulnerable to criticism / flawed because\\" (Flaw) |\\r\\n| PT107-S3-Q6 | Flaw | \\"a questionable assumption on which the argument relies\\" (Necessary Assumption) |\\r\\n| PT132-S2-Q1 | Most Supported | \\"principle best illustrated\\" (Principle-Conform) |\\r\\n\\r\\n## 8. Smaller slips spotted while reading\\r\\n\\r\\n- PT126-S1-Q2 \\"This fail to connect\\"; PT127-S2-Q8 \\"bolder\\"; PT127-S3-Q19 \\"Eric\\" for Erich; PT148-S3-Q19 \\"Panjak\\"; PT131-S2-Q5 \\"synthensized alphahyrdoxy\\"; PT124-S1-Q12 \\"lessons\\" for lessens; PT135-S4-Q6 copies the stimulus as \\"over- nitrified\\" (it says overnutrified); PT112-S1-Q9 \\"raising\\" for raisins and \\"per capita\\" for per calorie; PT107-S3-Q6 \\"is every badly weakened\\".\\r\\n- The question bank carries a \\"Games-y LR\\" feature tag (already noted 2026-09-26); worth renaming given the no-Logic-Games rule for student copy.\\r\\n"}'}]

## Tool: exec

text(await tools.mcp__codex_apps__lovable_query_database({...load("db"),sql:"select q.qid,q.question_type,q.prompt,q.stimulus,q.correct_choice,e.id explanation_id,jsonb_agg(distinct jsonb_build_object('id',s.id,'position',s.position,'label',s.label,'body',s.body,'md5',md5(s.body))) filter(where s.id is not null) steps,jsonb_agg(distinct jsonb_build_object('id',n.id,'letter',n.letter,'body',n.body,'md5',md5(n.body))) filter(where n.id is not null) notes from lr_questions q join lr_explanations e on e.question_id=q.id left join lr_explanation_steps s on s.explanation_id=e.id left join lr_explanation_choice_notes n on n.explanation_id=e.id where q.qid in ('PT108-S3-Q6','PT132-S2-Q3','PT126-S1-Q2','PT127-S3-Q19','PT131-S2-Q5','PT124-S1-Q12','PT135-S4-Q6','PT112-S1-Q9','PT107-S3-Q6') group by q.id,e.id;"}).then(v=>{store("step4InitialLive",v);const rows=v.structuredContent?.rows;text({questions:rows?.length});return ""; }));

const rows=load("step4InitialLive").structuredContent.rows;for(const r of rows){text({qid:r.qid,type:r.question_type,prompt:r.prompt,steps:r.steps.filter(s=>r.qid==="PT108-S3-Q6"||r.qid==="PT132-S2-Q3"||/fail to connect|Eric|synthens|alphahyr|lessons|over-\s*nitr|raising|per capita|every badly/i.test(s.body)),notes:r.notes.filter(s=>/fail to connect|Eric|synthens|alphahyr|lessons|over-\s*nitr|raising|per capita|every badly/i.test(s.body))});}

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"qid":"PT127-S3-Q19","type":"Main Conclusion","prompt":"Which one of the following most accurately expresses the main conclusion drawn in Leslie\'s argument?","steps":[],"notes":[{"body":"Whether or not Erich can be convinced is not discussed in Leslie’s argument.","id":"0fb60778-31be-4320-9bb7-ac90683a879e","letter":"D","md5":"13c9f980b2582437a4ac64afb20c4a85"},{"body":"Leslie’s argument assumes that Erich should not risk his physical well-being for the treasure, but this goes further and says that Erich should not risk his physical well-being for any possible gain.","id":"60fd84eb-ab5a-4b33-8ad5-2ebdd71dbb31","letter":"B","md5":"051d59663a1e97bfb38787a98d5ce300"}]}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"qid":"PT108-S3-Q6","type":"Paradox EXCEPT","prompt":"Each of the following, if true, could explain the decrease described above EXCEPT:","steps":[{"body":"The power plant opened and started discharging wastewater into the river.","id":"020f306b-a3e9-4046-8b8f-8e2726f19554","label":"Given that ...","md5":"c0d3870b52f4e43398903b56aedb04c7","position":0},{"body":"This isn\'t a true paradox. Nothing here is surprising. The stem just wants explanations for a drop in the number of bass caught.\\r\\n\\r\\nThink about what \\"number of bass caught\\" depends on. It takes two things: bass in the water and people fishing for them. So there are two roads to fewer bass caught:\\r\\n1. **Fewer bass downstream.** They died, got eaten, moved away, or lost their habitat.\\r\\n2. **Less fishing downstream.** Fewer anglers, or anglers fishing less.\\r\\n\\r\\nExpect most of the answers we eliminate to come in on road 1, with at least one sneaking in on road 2.\\r\\n\\r\\nMost students only think of road 1, because the wastewater feels like it must be hurting the fish. But road 2 is just as good. If half the anglers stop showing up, half as many bass get caught, even if the bass are thriving.","id":"30050c0b-3c74-407f-a7ab-42bd91a5178d","label":"Evaluate","md5":"c9fe331374bbbe17c7ac3f9ed1a95db6","position":2},{"body":"Eliminate four answers that explain fewer bass caught, by either road. The correct answer will do neither, or it will push the other way: more bass or more fishing, which should mean *more* bass caught.\\r\\n\\r\\nBe careful with any answer about anglers. It isn\'t out of scope. Anglers are half the equation.","id":"3a915989-8077-4179-8156-11839c6a5952","label":"Goal","md5":"3c26722b61516a64cf1806abf64423e2","position":3},{"body":"Shortly after, anglers downstream caught fewer bass overall?","id":"78fa0921-1787-4fe5-870a-f74547986e47","label":"Why is it that ...","md5":"27aa4f4da5027ff65c59223ce33f874d","position":1}],"notes":[]}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"qid":"PT135-S4-Q6","type":"Must Be True","prompt":"Which one of the following can be properly inferred from the information above?","steps":[],"notes":[]}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"qid":"PT107-S3-Q6","type":"Necessary Assumption","prompt":"Which one of the following is a questionable assumption on which the argument relies?","steps":[],"notes":[]}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"qid":"PT124-S1-Q12","type":"Flaw","prompt":"Jack\'s response most clearly trades on an ambiguity in which one of the following expressions used by Melinda?","steps":[],"notes":[]}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"qid":"PT132-S2-Q3","type":"Strengthen","prompt":"The conclusion is most strongly supported by the reasoning in the argument if which one of the following is assumed?","steps":[{"body":"Medications with an unpleasant taste are generally produced only in tablet, capsule, or soft-gel form.\\nPT → (T or C or SG)\\n\\nThe active ingredient in medication M is a waxy substance that cannot tolerate the heat used to manufacture tablets because it has a low melting point.\\n~T\\n\\nThe company developing M does not have soft-gel manufacturing technology and manufactures all its medications itself.\\n~SG","id":"41eff948-d99b-4ede-b6e1-9d513e0de2f8","label":"Evidence","md5":"41fbdb0fb68260cf91a839602ba59e59","position":0},{"body":"The stem says \\"if which one of the following is assumed\\", so we want the piece that makes the conclusion follow. The missing piece is the rule\'s trigger: M has an unpleasant taste. Once that\'s in place, the three-option rule applies, two options are gone, and capsule is the one left.","id":"a65bed50-5aa2-42be-9d04-2abc72aa56fd","label":"Goal","md5":"79bf7d390e41204b0242b1abc060d3f9","position":3},{"body":"M will most likely be produced in capsule form.\\nC","id":"bfdc099c-b881-48a9-a793-c558726d8a4a","label":"Conclusion","md5":"b80c1b4cfb978cb10c8fcbcdabfa84f8","position":1},{"body":"The conclusion follows a process of elimination. Medications with an unpleasant taste are \\"generally produced only in tablet, capsule, or soft-gel form.\\" M can\'t be a tablet, because its waxy ingredient can\'t take the heat. M can\'t be a soft-gel, because the company \\"does not have soft-gel manufacturing technology\\" and makes everything itself. Capsule is what\'s left.\\n\\nBut look at who the first rule covers: only medications with an unpleasant taste. Nothing tells us M tastes bad. If M tastes fine, the rule never applies, and M could come in a liquid, a powder, or anything else. The elimination only works if M is in the group the rule covers.\\nPT → (T or C or SG)\\n~T\\n~SG\\nmissing: PT","id":"dfe89858-b232-4a53-b9da-12973ec59c10","label":"Evaluate","md5":"6594fb8982722c5f1925afab0e0c4adf","position":2}],"notes":[]}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"qid":"PT131-S2-Q5","type":"Strengthen","prompt":"Which one of the following, if true, would most strengthen Sabina\'s argument?","steps":[],"notes":[]}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"qid":"PT126-S1-Q2","type":"Necessary Assumption","prompt":"Which one of the following is an assumption required by the argument?","steps":[],"notes":[]}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"qid":"PT112-S1-Q9","type":"Paradox","prompt":"Which one of the following, if true, most helps to explain why raisins contain more iron per calorie than do grapes?","steps":[],"notes":[]}'}]

Verified merged at 2026-09-27 06:51 UTC with `gh pr view` on 2026-09-28.\\r\\n- [ ] **Bank misalignment:** PT119-S2-Q19 is matched to a different question, and PT101-S3-Q1\'s bank text differs from the DB. Detail: [[side-issues]] section 3.\\r\\n- [ ] **Transcription damage, LSAT Lab leftovers, and small typos in explanations.** Detail: [[side-issues]] sections 5, 6 and 8.\\r\\n- [ ] **Rename the \\"Games-y LR\\" feature tag.** There are no Logic Games on the LSAT.\\r\\n- [x] **Question bank `questionType` is unreliable.** 110 raw variants, casing duplicates, some wrong (PT151-S2-Q13 is a Paradox, bank says Most Strongly Supported; PT143-S1-Q6 is a Paradox, bank says Strengthen). Needs a canonical ~25-type set. **Fixed 2026-10-03** (type sweep, see below).\\r\\n- [ ] **110 explained questions have no match in the question bank** (`public/data/PT*-LR.json`), so they have no features or difficulty.\\r\\n- [ ] **Reasoning-type names don\'t match the glossary.** Data: \\"Conditional Reasoning\\". Glossary: \\"Conditional Logic\\".\\r\\n- [ ] **PT123 to PT138 are thin.** 362 words on average, 51% Complete. Check whether some \\"Complete\\" ones are too thin to count.\\r\\n- [ ] **Diagrams lose their layout in the database.** PT107-S3-Q5 (chain flattened to inline text), PT146-S1-Q21 (transcription damage). Count how many render badly in the app.\\r\\n\\r\\n### App and loading\\r\\n- [ ] **The app has two explanation paths.** `useLrExplanation` reads the database; `questionLoader.mergeExplanationSidecars` also fetches `public/data/explanations/PT<n>-explanations.json`, built from the local markdown for PT139 to PT159. Find out which one students see, and retire the sidecars if the database has replaced them. Until then, the PT140 sidecar JSON still carries the thin text.\\r\\n- [x] ~~**Where the LSAT U writer mark (Explanation Source) lives.**~~ 2026-09-27: Joshua dropped it; not needed.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '[{"qid":"PT108-S3-Q6","id":"30050c0b-3c74-407f-a7ab-42bd91a5178d","old":"c9fe331374bbbe17c7ac3f9ed1a95db6","new":"1ccdbd3ca089eac750b68e5cf4223598"},{"qid":"PT108-S3-Q6","id":"3a915989-8077-4179-8156-11839c6a5952","old":"3c26722b61516a64cf1806abf64423e2","new":"2a912b4ff39edb7a272df9230230e31a"}]'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'undefined'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 7.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"rows":[{"choice_d":[{"id":"75ee6f0d-1181-4f16-90d8-cb8c768d6e42","letter":"D","tag":"Strengthens","text":"Some of the subjects who were given placebos consumed foods during the study that are naturally rich in vitamin C."}],"correct_choice":"A","explanation_id":"b378e1b0-88e3-450f-9bdf-10644a048ea1","notes_d":[{"body":"The placebo group was eating vitamin C-rich foods? If anything, that helps the argument. If both groups were getting vitamin C (one through pills, one through diet) and there was no difference, that is more evidence vitamin C does not help with acne. This answer accidentally supports the conclusion instead of weakening it.","id":"fa758a01-be00-4123-be04-47a3fdc10084","letter":"D","md5":"992dbdb6768119db9ba315d2bb9621cb"}],"prompt":"Which one of the following, if true, most seriously weakens the argument?","qid":"PT157-S3-Q25","stimulus":"To test the claim that vitamin C is effective in treating acne, scientists administered it to one group of subjects and a placebo to a control group. The group receiving vitamin C had less severe acne during the study than did the control group. It was subsequently discovered, however, that half of the subjects in each group knew which kind of pill they were given. text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/corpus/lr-explanations/fixes/step4-2026-10-10/update-inventory.py\n+\"\"\"Correct only PT119-S2-Q19's obsolete bank match, difficulty and features.\"\"\"\n+from pathlib import Path\n+import csv\n+import hashlib\n+import io\n+import json\n+import sys\n+\n+root = Path(__file__).resolve().parent\n+inventory = root.parent / 'revamp-inventory.csv'\n+before = inventory.read_bytes()\n+lines = before.decode('utf-8').splitlines(keepends=True)\n+header = next(csv.reader([lines[0]]))\n+matches = [i for i, line in enumerate(lines[1:], 1) if next(csv.reader([line]))[0] == 'PT119-S2-Q19']\n+if len(matches) != 1:\n+    raise RuntimeError('Expected one inventory row')\n+index = matches[0]\n+old_row = dict(zip(header, next(csv.reader([lines[index]]))))\n+expected = {'bank_match': 'moved:PT103-S2-Q24', 'difficulty': '3', 'features': '2-Speaker; Causal Difference-Maker; Principles in Answers'}\n+if any(old_row[k] != v for k, v in expected.items()):\n+    raise RuntimeError('Inventory row changed; stop')\n+bank = next(x['question'] for x in json.loads((root / 'live-bank-source.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8')) if x['qid'] == 'PT119-S2-Q19')\n+db = next(x for x in json.loads((root / 'initial-live-snapshot.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))['sources']['structuredContent']['rows'] if x['qid'] == 'PT119-S2-Q19')\n+if bank['stimulus'] != db['stimulus'] or bank['questionStem'] != db['prompt'] or bank['correctAnswer'] != db['correct_choice'] or any(bank['answerChoices'][c['letter']] != c['text'] for c in db['choices']):\n+    raise RuntimeError('Bank and live DB do not match')\n+new_fields = {'bank_match': 'Y', 'difficulty': str(bank['questionDifficulty']), 'features': '; '.join(bank['questionFeatures'])}\n+new_row = {**old_row, **new_fields}\n+stream = io.StringIO(newline='')\n+csv.writer(stream, lineterminator='\\r\\n' if lines[index].endswith('\\r\\n') else '\\n').writerow([new_row[k] for k in header])\n+lines[index] = stream.getvalue()\n+after = ''.join(lines).encode('utf-8')\n+receipt = {'qid': db['qid'], 'old_fields': expected, 'new_fields': new_fields, 'unchanged_fields': all(old_row[k] == new_row[k] for k in header if k not in new_fields), 'before_sha256': hashlib.sha256(before).hexdigest(), 'after_sha256': hashlib.sha256(after).hexdigest(), 'applied': '--apply' in sys.argv}\n+if receipt['applied']:\n+    snapshot = root / 'revamp-inventory-before.csv'\n+    if snapshot.exists() and snapshot.read_bytes() != before:\n+        raise RuntimeError('Existing snapshot differs')\n+    snapshot.write_bytes(before)\n+    if inventory.read_bytes() != before:\n+        raise RuntimeError('Inventory changed during preparation')\n+    inventory.write_bytes(after)\n+    if inventory.read_bytes() != after:\n+        raise RuntimeError('Inventory readback differs')\n+    (root / 'inventory-receipt.json').write_bytes((json.dumps(receipt, indent=2) + '\\n').encode('utf-8'))\n+print(json.dumps(receipt))\n*** End Patch"));

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 4.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"13740e","wall_time_seconds":0.4104105,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":829,"output":"---\\r\\nname: wrapup\\r\\ndescription: Close an LSAT U session by updating the Obsidian vault so the next chat picks up cleanly. Marks finished tasks done (struck through) in the task ledger, refreshes the handoff cache, and appends to the log. Trigger with /wrapup.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nClose the current LSAT U / Aspiring Attorneys session. Leave the next session a clean, accurate handoff and mark completed work as done so it is never re-run. Resolve the vault root from the current workspace and its nearest instruction files; never assume a saved `~/ai-os/`, Windows, or Mac path. In this vault, read `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` and obey its index/log conventions.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Step 0: read the anchor\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore writing anything, re-read `wiki/tasks.md` and `wiki/hot.md` so the handoff reflects the real goal, not just the last thing touched. If a `plans/` file or README framed this session, skim it too.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Step 1: confirm task status changes only when needed\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom verified session work, determine whether task status changes are needed. If no task status changes are needed, leave `wiki/tasks.md` alone and continue with the authorized handoff and log updates without a confirmation step. If task status changes are proposed, show Joshua the concrete proposal before editing `wiki/tasks.md`:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nProposing for wiki/tasks.md:\\r\\n  -> Done (strike through): <items you believe are finished this session>\\r\\n  -> Stays in Now: <still in progress>\\r\\n  -> New Next: <anything surfaced that should be queued>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nWait for Joshua to approve or correct proposed task status changes, unless those exact changes were already approved in this session. Completion MUST be correct: a wrong \\"Done\\" is the exact failure this prevents. Do not mark something done on a guess; if unsure, leave its status unchanged and say so. This gate applies to task status changes, not to routine authorized handoff/log filing. If requesting approval, explain that this skill requires confirmation before changing task status.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Step 2: write the authorized handoff and log\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **`wiki/tasks.md`** - move approved finished items into `## Done`, wrapping each in `~~...~~` with a date, e.g. `~~Wire AI tutor into official drill surface~~ (2026-06-12)`. Update `## Now` and `## Next` only as approved. Preserve older task records; do not prune or delete them without explicit authorization. Skip this file when no task changes were approved or needed.\\r\\n2. **`wiki/hot.md`** - rewrite the `## Most recent (YYYY-MM-DD): <title>` block with 4 to 8 bullets of what changed. Refresh `## Active priorities` and `## Open threads` so they are true now.\\r\\n3. **`wiki/log.md`** - append a new entry at the BOTTOM of the log using `## [YYYY-MM-DD] system | Session wrapup`. Include source/output links, meaningful changed pages, verified results, unresolved work, and any skipped steps. Use Joshua\'s client date/time context. Preserve old entries exactly, including their legacy formats; never rewrite or reorder them. Multiple sessions per day are normal, so add a distinct title suffix when useful. If this session\'s authorized handoff and log entry already contain the same verified work, report them rather than duplicating the entry.\\r\\n4. **Task anchor** - if a README, plan, or wiki page advanced, make a surgical status update. Touch only what changed.\\r\\n"}'}]

next(r for r in rows(load(\'q157-styles-live.json\')) if r[\'style\'] == \'standard\')[\'notes_d\'][0]\\r\\nsql = \'with expected_questions(qid,rid,stimulus_md5,prompt_md5,key) as (values\\\\n\' + values(question_values) + \'),\\\\n\'\\r\\nsql += \'expected_choices(qid,rid,letter,body_md5,correct) as (values\\\\n\' + values(choice_values) + \'),\\\\n\'\\r\\nsql += \'expected_edits(qid,tbl,col,rid,eid,slot,label,old_text,old_md5,new_text,new_md5) as (values\\\\n\' + values(edit_values) + \'),\\\\n\'\\r\\nsql += \\"\\"\\"\\r\\nlocked_questions as materialized(select t.* from lr_questions t where t.qid in(select qid from expected_questions) for update),\\r\\nlocked_choices as materialized(select t.* from lr_answer_choices t where t.question_id in(select id from locked_questions) for update),\\r\\nlocked_explanations as materialized(select t.* from lr_explanations t where t.question_id in(select id from locked_questions) for update),\\r\\nlocked_steps as materialized(select t.* from lr_explanation_steps t where t.id::text in(select rid from expected_edits where tbl=\'lr_explanation_steps\') for update),\\r\\nlocked_notes as materialized(select t.* from lr_explanation_choice_notes t where t.id::text in(select rid from expected_edits where tbl=\'lr_explanation_choice_notes\') or t.id::text=STANDARD_ID for update),\\r\\nactual as (\\r\\n select q.qid,\'lr_questions\'::text tbl,\'stimulus\'::text col,q.id::text rid,null::text eid,\'\'::text slot,null::text label,q.stimulus body from locked_questions q\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_answer_choices\',\'tag\',c.id::text,null,c.letter::text,null,c.tag from locked_choices c join locked_questions q on q.id=c.question_id\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_answer_choices\',\'choice_text\',c.id::text,null,c.letter::text,null,c.choice_text from locked_choices c join locked_questions q on q.id=c.question_id\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_explanation_steps\',\'body\',s.id::text,e.id::text,s.position::text,s.label,s.body from locked_steps s join locked_explanations e on e.id=s.explanation_id join locked_questions q on q.id=e.question_id\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_explanation_choice_notes\',\'body\',n.id::text,e.id::text,n.letter::text,null,n.body from locked_notes n join locked_explanations e on e.id=n.explanation_id join locked_questions q on q.id=e.question_id),\\r\\neligibility as materialized(select\\r\\n not exists(select 1 from expected_questions x where (select count(*) from locked_questions t where t.id::text=x.rid and t.qid=x.qid and md5(t.stimulus)=x.stimulus_md5 and md5(t.prompt)=x.prompt_md5 and t.correct_choice::text=x.key)<>1)\\r\\n and not exists(select 1 from expected_choices x where (select count(*) from locked_choices c join locked_questions q on q.id=c.question_id where q.qid=x.qid and c.id::text=x.rid and c.letter::text=x.letter and md5(c.choice_text)=x.body_md5 and c.is_correct=x.correct::boolean)<>1)\\r\\n and not exists(select 1 from locked_choices c join locked_questions q on q.id=c.question_id where not exists(select 1 from expected_choices x where x.qid=q.qid and x.rid=c.id::text))\\r\\n and not exists(select 1 from expected_edits e where (select count(*) from actual a where a.qid=e.qid and a.tbl=e.tbl and a.col=e.col and a.rid=e.rid and (e.eid is null or a.eid=e.eid) and a.slot=e.slot and (e.label is null or a.label=e.label) and a.body=e.old_text and md5(a.body)=e.old_md5)<>1)\\r\\n and (select count(*) from locked_notes n where n.id::text=STANDARD_ID and md5(n.body)=STANDARD_MD5)=1 eligible)\\r\\n\\"\\"\\".replace(\'STANDARD_ID\', quote(standard[\'id\'])).replace(\'STANDARD_MD5\', quote(standard[\'md5\']))\\r\\n(ROOT / \'preflight.sql\').write_bytes((sql + \'select eligible from eligibility;\\\\n\').encode(\'utf-8\'))\\r\\n\\r\\nupdates = []\\r\\nfor i, edit in enumerate(edits):\\r\\n    if edit[\'table\'] == \'lr_questions\':\\r\\n        ownership = \'t.qid=\' + quote(edit[\'qid\'])\\r\\n    elif edit[\'table\'] == \'lr_answer_choices\':\\r\\n        ownership = \'exists(select 1 from locked_questions q where q.id=t.question_id and q.qid=\' + quote(edit[\'qid\']) + \')\'\\r\\n    else:\\r\\n        ownership = \'t.explanation_id=\' + quote(edit[\'explanation_id\']) + \'::uuid and exists(select 1 from locked_explanations x join locked_questions q on q.id=x.question_id where x.id=t.explanation_id and q.qid=\' + quote(edit[\'qid\']) + \')\'\\r\\n    if edit[\'table\'] == \'lr_explanation_steps\':\\r\\n        ownership += \' and t.position=\' + str(edit[\'position\']) + \' and t.label=\' + quote(edit[\'label\'])\\r\\n    if edit[\'table\'] in (\'lr_answer_choices\', \'lr_explanation_choice_notes\'):\\r\\n        ownership += \' and t.letter::text=\' + quote(edit[\'letter\'])\\r\\n    updates.append(\'u\' + str(i) + \' as (update \' + edit[\'table\'] + \' t set \' + edit[\'column\'] + \'=\' + quote(edit[\'new\']) + \' from eligibility g where g.eligible and t.id=\' + quote(edit[\'row_id\']) + \'::uuid and \' + ownership + \' and t.\' + edit[\'column\'] + \'=\' + quote(edit[\'old\']) + \' and md5(t.\' + edit[\'column\'] + \')=\' + quote(edit[\'old_md5\']) + \' returning t.id::text rid,\' + quote(edit[\'table\']) + \'::text tbl,md5(t.\' + edit[\'column\'] + \') body_md5)\')\\r\\nsql += \',\' + \',\\\\n\'.join(updates)\\r\\nsql += \',writes as (\' + \' union all \'.join(\'select * from u\' + str(i) for i in range(len(edits))) + \'),\'\\r\\nsql += \\"\\"\\"\\r\\nresult as(select (select eligible from eligibility) eligible,(select count(*) from writes) updated_rows,\\r\\n (select count(*) from writes w join expected_edits e on e.rid=w.rid and e.tbl=w.tbl and e.new_md5=w.body_md5) verified_rows,\\r\\n (select jsonb_agg(to_jsonb(w)) from writes w) receipts)\\r\\nselect *,1/(case when (not eligible and updated_rows=0) or (eligible and updated_rows=16 and verified_rows=16) then 1 else 0 end) atomic_assertion from result;\\r\\n\\"\\"\\"\\r\\n(ROOT / \'apply-atomic.sql\').write_bytes(sql.encode(\'utf-8\'))\\r\\nprint(\'Built 16-edit atomic SQL and read-only preflight, LF bytes only\')\\r\\n"}'}]

q\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_answer_choices\',\'tag\',c.id::text,null,c.letter::text,null,c.tag from locked_choices c join locked_questions q on q.id=c.question_id\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_answer_choices\',\'choice_text\',c.id::text,null,c.letter::text,null,c.choice_text from locked_choices c join locked_questions q on q.id=c.question_id\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_explanation_steps\',\'body\',s.id::text,e.id::text,s.position::text,s.label,s.body from locked_steps s join locked_explanations e on e.id=s.explanation_id join locked_questions q on q.id=e.question_id\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_explanation_choice_notes\',\'body\',n.id::text,e.id::text,n.letter::text,null,n.body from locked_notes n join locked_explanations e on e.id=n.explanation_id join locked_questions q on q.id=e.question_id),\\r\\neligibility as materialized(select\\r\\n not exists(select 1 from expected_questions x where (select count(*) from locked_questions t where t.id::text=x.rid and t.qid=x.qid and md5(t.stimulus)=x.stimulus_md5 and md5(t.prompt)=x.prompt_md5 and t.correct_choice::text=x.key)<>1)\\r\\n and not exists(select 1 from expected_choices x where (select count(*) from locked_choices c join locked_questions q on q.id=c.question_id where q.qid=x.qid and c.id::text=x.rid and c.letter::text=x.letter and md5(c.choice_text)=x.body_md5 and c.is_correct=x.correct::boolean)<>1)\\r\\n and not exists(select 1 from locked_choices c join locked_questions q on q.id=c.question_id where not exists(select 1 from expected_choices x where x.qid=q.qid and x.rid=c.id::text))\\r\\n and not exists(select 1 from expected_edits e where (select count(*) from actual a where a.qid=e.qid and a.tbl=e.tbl and a.col=e.col and a.rid=e.rid and (e.eid is null or a.eid=e.eid) and a.slot=e.slot and (e.label is null or a.label=e.label) and a.body=e.old_text and md5(a.body)=e.old_md5)<>1)\\r\\n and (select count(*) from locked_notes n where n.id::text=STANDARD_ID and md5(n.body)=STANDARD_MD5)=1 eligible)\\r\\n\\"\\"\\".replace(\'STANDARD_ID\', quote(standard[\'id\'])).replace(\'STANDARD_MD5\', quote(standard[\'md5\']))\\r\\n(ROOT / \'preflight.sql\').write_bytes((sql + \'select eligible from eligibility;\\\\n\').encode(\'utf-8\'))\\r\\n\\r\\nupdates = []\\r\\nfor i, edit in enumerate(edits):\\r\\n    if edit[\'table\'] == \'lr_questions\':\\r\\n        ownership = \'t.qid=\' + quote(edit[\'qid\'])\\r\\n    elif edit[\'table\'] == \'lr_answer_choices\':\\r\\n        ownership = \'exists(select 1 from locked_questions q where q.id=t.question_id and q.qid=\' + quote(edit[\'qid\']) + \')\'\\r\\n    else:\\r\\n        ownership = \'t.explanation_id=\' + quote(edit[\'explanation_id\']) + \'::uuid and exists(select 1 from locked_explanations x join locked_questions q on q.id=x.question_id where x.id=t.explanation_id and q.qid=\' + quote(edit[\'qid\']) + \')\'\\r\\n    if edit[\'table\'] == \'lr_explanation_steps\':\\r\\n        ownership += \' and t.position=\' + str(edit[\'position\']) + \' and t.label=\' + quote(edit[\'label\'])\\r\\n    if edit[\'table\'] in (\'lr_answer_choices\', \'lr_explanation_choice_notes\'):\\r\\n        ownership += \' and t.letter::text=\' + quote(edit[\'letter\'])\\r\\n    updates.append(\'u\' + str(i) + \' as (update \' + edit[\'table\'] + \' t set \' + edit[\'column\'] + \'=\' + quote(edit[\'new\']) + \' from eligibility g where g.eligible and t.id=\' + quote(edit[\'row_id\']) + \'::uuid and \' + ownership + \' and t.\' + edit[\'column\'] + \'=\' + quote(edit[\'old\']) + \' and md5(t.\' + edit[\'column\'] + \')=\' + quote(edit[\'old_md5\']) + \' returning t.id::text rid,\' + quote(edit[\'table\']) + \'::text tbl,md5(t.\' + edit[\'column\'] + \') body_md5)\')\\r\\nsql += \',\' + \',\\\\n\'.join(updates)\\r\\nsql += \',writes as (\' + \' union all \'.join(\'select * from u\' + str(i) for i in range(len(edits))) + \'),\'\\r\\nsql += \\"\\"\\"\\r\\nresult as(select (select eligible from eligibility) eligible,(select count(*) from writes) updated_rows,\\r\\n (select count(*) from writes w join expected_edits e on e.rid=w.rid and e.tbl=w.tbl and e.new_md5=w.body_md5) verified_rows,\\r\\n (select jsonb_agg(to_jsonb(w)) from writes w) receipts)\\r\\nselect *,1/(case when (not eligible and updated_rows=0) or (eligible and updated_rows=16 and verified_rows=16) then 1 else 0 end) atomic_assertion from result;\\r\\n\\"\\"\\"\\r\\n(ROOT / \'apply-atomic.sql\').write_bytes(sql.encode(\'utf-8\'))\\r\\n\\r\\nnew_questions = [row.copy() for row in question_values]\\r\\nnew_choices = [row.copy() for row in choice_values]\\r\\nfor edit in edits:\\r\\n    if edit[\'table\'] == \'lr_questions\':\\r\\n        next(row for row in new_questions if row[1] == edit[\'row_id\'])[2] = edit[\'new_md5\']\\r\\n    if edit[\'table\'] == \'lr_answer_choices\' and edit[\'column\'] == \'choice_text\':\\r\\n        next(row for row in new_choices if row[1] == edit[\'row_id\'])[3] = edit[\'new_md5\']\\r\\n\\r\\naudit = \'with expected_questions(qid,rid,stimulus_md5,prompt_md5,key) as (values\\\\n\' + values(new_questions) + \'),\\\\n\'\\r\\naudit += \'expected_choices(qid,rid,letter,body_md5,correct) as (values\\\\n\' + values(new_choices) + \'),\\\\n\'\\r\\naudit += \'expected_edits(qid,tbl,col,rid,eid,slot,label,old_text,old_md5,new_text,new_md5) as (values\\\\n\' + values(edit_values) + \'),\\\\n\'\\r\\naudit += \\"\\"\\"\\r\\nactual as (\\r\\n select q.qid,\'lr_questions\'::text tbl,\'stimulus\'::text col,q.id::text rid,null::text eid,\'\'::text slot,null::text label,q.stimulus body from lr_questions q where q.qid in(select qid from expected_questions)\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_answer_choices\',\'tag\',c.id::text,null,c.letter::text,null,c.tag from lr_answer_choices c join lr_questions q on q.id=c.question_id where c.id::text in(select rid from expected_edits)\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_answer_choices\',\'choice_text\',c.id::text,null,c.letter::text,null,c.choice_text from lr_answer_choices c join lr_questions q on q.id=c.question_id where c.id::text in(select rid from expected_edits)\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_explanation_steps\',\'body\',s.id::text,e.id::text,s.position::text,s.label,s.body from lr_explanation_steps s join lr_explanations e on e.id=s.explanation_id join lr_questions q on q.id=e.question_id where s.id::text in(select rid from expected_edits)\\r\\n union all select q.qid,\'lr_explanation_choice_notes\',\'body\',n.id::text,e.id::text,n.letter::text,null,n.body from lr_explanation_choice_notes n join lr_explanations e on e.id=n.explanation_id join lr_questions q on q.id=e.question_id where n.id::text in(select rid from expected_edits)),\\r\\nchecked as (select e.qid,e.tbl,e.col,e.rid,\\r\\n (select count(*) from actual a where a.qid=e.qid and a.tbl=e.tbl and a.col=e.col and a.rid=e.rid and (e.eid is null or a.eid=e.eid) and a.slot=e.slot and (e.label is null or a.label=e.label) and a.body=e.new_text and md5(a.body)=e.new_md5) matches from expected_edits e),\\r\\nquestion_checks as (select x.qid,\\r\\n (select count(*) from lr_questions t where t.id::text=x.rid and t.qid=x.qid and md5(t.stimulus)=x.stimulus_md5 and md5(t.prompt)=x.prompt_md5 and t.correct_choice::text=x.key) matches from expected_questions x),\\r\\nchoice_checks as (select x.qid,x.letter,\\r\\n (select count(*) from lr_answer_choices c join lr_questions q on q.id=c.question_id where q.qid=x.qid and c.id::text=x.rid and c.letter::text=x.letter and md5(c.choice_text)=x.body_md5 and c.is_correct=x.correct::boolean) matches from expected_choices x)\\r\\nselect jsonb_build_object(\'expected_rows\',16,\'matching_rows\',(select count(*) from checked where matches=1),\\r\\n \'mismatches\',coalesce((select jsonb_agg(to_jsonb(c)) from checked c where matches<>1),\'[]\'::jsonb),\\r\\n \'question_snapshot_mismatches\',coalesce((select jsonb_agg(to_jsonb(c)) from question_checks c where matches<>1),\'[]\'::jsonb),\\r\\n \'choice_snapshot_mismatches\',coalesce((select jsonb_agg(to_jsonb(c)) from choice_checks c where matches<>1),\'[]\'::jsonb),\\r\\n \'extra_choice_rows\',(select count(*) from lr_answer_choices c join lr_questions q on q.id=c.question_id where q.qid in(select qid from expected_questions) and not exists(select 1 from expected_choices x where x.rid=c.id::text)),\\r\\n \'standard_preserved\',(select count(*) from lr_explanation_choice_notes n join lr_explanations e on e.id=n.explanation_id join lr_questions q on q.id=e.question_id where n.id::text=STANDARD_ID and md5(n.body)=STANDARD_MD5 and q.qid=E\'PT157-S3-Q25\' and e.style=E\'standard\')) audit;\\r\\n\\"\\"\\".replace(\'STANDARD_ID\', quote(standard[\'id\'])).replace(\'STANDARD_MD5\', quote(standard[\'md5\']))\\r\\n(ROOT / \'read-after-audit.sql\').write_bytes(audit.encode(\'utf-8\'))\\r\\nassert all(b\'\\\\r\' not in (ROOT / name).read_bytes() for name in [\'apply-atomic.sql\', \'preflight.sql\', \'read-after-audit.sql\'])\\r\\nprint(\'Built 16-edit atomic SQL, preflight and read-after audit; content newlines escaped in E literals\')\\r\\n"}'}]

lr_answer_choices t set choice_text=e.new_text from expected_edits e,eligibility g\\n where g.eligible and t.id=e.rid::uuid and t.question_id=e.qid_uuid::uuid and t.letter::text=e.letter\\n and t.choice_text=e.old_text and md5(t.choice_text)=e.old_md5\\n and exists(select 1 from locked_questions q where q.id=t.question_id and q.qid=e.qid)\\n returning t.id::text rid,md5(t.choice_text) body_md5),\\nresult as(select (select eligible from eligibility) eligible,(select count(*) from updated) updated_rows,\\n (select count(*) from updated u join expected_edits e on e.rid=u.rid and e.new_md5=u.body_md5) verified_rows,\\n (select jsonb_agg(to_jsonb(u)) from updated u) receipts)\\nselect *,1/(case when (not eligible and updated_rows=0) or (eligible and updated_rows=2 and
